Use when the user runs /design-sync or says \"sync my design system to Claude Design\".","argumentHint":"[<project hint, e.g. \"Acme DS\">]","builtin":true},{"name":"dataviz","description":"Use this skill whenever you are about to create ANY chart, graph, plot, dashboard, or data visualization, in ANY output medium — an HTML or React artifact, inline SVG, plotting code in any library (matplotlib, plotly, d3, Recharts, …), an image/PNG you will render and upload, or a chart shared into Slack. Read it BEFORE writing the first line of chart code, choosing chart colors, building a stat tile / meter / KPI row, or laying out a dashboard. When the destination is a first-party document connector (host-designated, never self-described) that renders live charts, hand it the rows (inline, or as an uploaded data file the chart cites) rather than a rendered PNG/SVG — a picture of a chart loses hover, data inspection and per-value comments. Produces visualizations that read as one system — elegant, accessible, consistent in light and dark — using a brand-neutral placeholder palette you swap for your own. Teaches a design-system-agnostic method: a form heuristic, a color formula with a runnable validator, mark specs, and interaction rules. A validated default palette is documented in `references/palette.md` — swap that file's values for your brand's. Triggers on: \"chart\", \"graph\", \"plot\", \"data viz\", \"visualization\", \"dashboard\", \"analytics\", \"visualize data\", \"categorical colors\", \"sequential / diverging palette\", \"stat tile\", \"sparkline\", \"heatmap\", \"legend\", \"axis\", \"tooltip\", \"chart colors\", \"color by series\".","argumentHint":"","builtin":true},{"name":"update-config","description":"Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.","argumentHint":"","builtin":true},{"name":"verify","description":"Verify that a code change actually does what it's supposed to by exercising it end-to-end and observing behavior — drive the affected flow, not just tests or typecheck. Run before committing nontrivial changes; bootstraps this repo's project verify skill if none exists yet. Don't invoke it on a diff that only touches tests, docs, or other code with no runtime surface to drive (a change to product source always has one) — there's nothing to observe.","argumentHint":"","builtin":true},{"name":"debug","description":"Enable debug logging for this session and help diagnose issues","argumentHint":"[issue description]","builtin":true},{"name":"code-review","description":"Review the current diff, or a PR number/branch/path target, for correctness bugs (plus reuse/simplification/efficiency cleanups where the model's review recipe covers them) at the given effort level (low/medium: fewer, high-confidence findings; high→max: broader coverage, may include uncertain findings; ultra: deep multi-agent review in the cloud); with no level given, it reuses the level you typed last. Pass --comment to post findings as inline PR comments, or --fix to apply the findings to the working tree after the review. For ultra on a GitHub.com PR target, --post asks to post the finished review’s findings to the PR as a single comment from the user’s GitHub account (not a review; the launch dialog still confirms in interactive sessions, while non-interactive mode posts on the flag alone) and --no-post hides that option.","argumentHint":"[low|medium|high|xhigh|max|ultra] [--fix] [--comment] [<pr#>|<branch>|<path>]","aliases":["review"],"builtin":true},{"name":"simplify","description":"Review the changed code for reuse, simplification, efficiency, and altitude cleanups, then apply the fixes. Quality only — it does not hunt for bugs; use /code-review for that.","argumentHint":"[<target>]","builtin":true},{"name":"batch","description":"Research and plan a large-scale change, then execute it in parallel across 5–30 isolated worktree agents that each open a PR.","argumentHint":"<instruction>","builtin":true},{"name":"fewer-permission-prompts","description":"Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.","argumentHint":"","builtin":true},{"name":"doctor","description":"Health-check the user's Claude Code setup and fix issues: diagnose installation health — what the `claude doctor` terminal diagnostics cover — from local data (duplicate or leftover installs, PATH, unparseable settings files, broken or colliding agent definitions, skills whose frontmatter fails to parse); find unused skills, MCP servers, and plugins versus their context cost and disable dead weight; deduplicate local CLAUDE.md files against checked-in ones; trim checked-in CLAUDE.md files by cutting content a session could derive from the codebase (directory layouts, tech-stack lists, architecture overviews) while keeping gotchas, rationale, and non-standard conventions; migrate always-loaded CLAUDE.md guidance into lazy skills and nested CLAUDE.md files; flag slow hooks and context-heavy extensions; check the installed version is current; make auto mode the default permission mode; and pre-approve frequently denied read-only commands. Use when the user asks for a doctor run, checkup, audit, tune-up, or cleanup of their Claude Code setup or configuration.","argumentHint":"","aliases":["checkup"],"builtin":true},{"name":"loop","description":"Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). and mcp__remote-devices__Claude_Browser__* when a cloud session is linked to the person's computer; before those tools are turned on there may be a single enable__mcp__remote-devices__Claude_Browser tool instead. It covers the pane's persistent sign-ins, tabs and preview_start, reading pages as text, site approvals, what the pane cannot open, and what to do when it cannot be reached. It is not for Claude in Chrome (mcp__claude-in-chrome__* tools), which has its own skill, and it does not decide which browser to use. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["built-in-browser"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:chrome-browser","description":"Read this skill before the first step that uses Claude in Chrome, the browser extension whose tools are named mcp__claude-in-chrome__* (also called Chrome, the browser extension, or the external browser) and which acts in the person's real Chrome with their own sign-ins; before those tools are turned on there may be a single enable__mcp__claude-in-chrome tool instead. It covers loading the tools in one ToolSearch call, checking the person's open tabs and working in a new tab, site permissions, GIF recordings, console logs, dialogs to avoid, and when to stop and ask. It is not for the built-in browser (mcp__Claude_Browser__* or mcp__remote-devices__Claude_Browser__* tools), which has its own skill, and it does not decide which browser to use. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["chrome-browser"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:computer-use","description":"Read this skill before the first step of any request to do something in an app on the person's own computer (Notes, Finder, System Settings, any desktop app), to look at their screen, or for \"computer use\". Computer use (desktop control) lets Claude take screenshots of the person's desktop and control it with clicks, typing and scrolling through the Claude desktop app; its tools are named mcp__computer-use__* when the session runs in the desktop app and mcp__remote-devices__computer_* when a cloud session is linked to the person's computer; before computer use is turned on for a conversation there may be no such tools, only an enable__mcp__remote-devices__computer tool, which turns it on. It covers turning it on, picking the right tool, the access flow, and the safety rules for tiered apps, links and financial actions. It is not for websites, which go through Claude in Chrome or the built-in browser and their own skills. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["computer-use"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:deep-research","description":"Use this skill when the user's prompt requires (1) researching a topic across multiple sources, comparing options or alternatives, analyzing trends or history, understanding markets or industries, or reviewing literature or studies and (2) synthesizing that research into a comprehensive, narrative report. If you're planning to search the web or internal knowledge bases, consider using this skill. This skill coordinates research subagents, so use it only when you have a tool for spawning subagents (the Agent or Task tool); otherwise, research the question directly. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":""},{"name":"anthropic-skills:docs","description":"docs (living docs people share, comment on and edit; use only when the user asks for one: names a doc, document, page, memo, spec, PRD, runbook or write-up, asks for somewhere to share or keep editing something, or says yes to your doc offer; a plan, comparison, summary or notes asked in chat stays in chat (at most a one-line doc offer); a report, status update, recap or \"something I can send them\" with no form named → ask first: reply, doc or file?; tabs hold tables and live charts too; a pasted claude.ai/code/artifact/… link may be a doc: check with docs tools first; not HTML pages, apps or plain chat answers; a .docx/.pptx/.xlsx/PDF asked for by name → that format's skill): asked for one → no docs-connector instructions in context? call the docs connector's `guide` with topic.instructions first, then create the doc (headings only, no body) before any search, file read or plan, even with files attached. Documenting code means docstrings or repo docs, not a doc. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["docs"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:docx","description":"Use this skill whenever the user wants to create, read, edit, or manipulate Word documents (.docx) or Word templates (.dotx). Triggers include: any mention of 'Word doc', 'word document', '.docx', '.dotx', or requests to produce professional documents with formatting like tables of contents, page numbers, or letterheads. Also use when extracting or reorganizing content from .docx or .dotx files, inserting or replacing images in documents, find-and-replace in Word files, working with tracked changes or comments, or converting content into a polished Word document. If the user asks for a 'report', 'memo', 'letter', 'template', or similar deliverable as a Word or .docx file (to download, email or print), use this skill. However, if they ask for a document, page, report, memo, or notes WITHOUT naming a file format and the session offers Claude's own dedicated document or page skill or connector, use that instead. Do NOT use for PDFs, spreadsheets, Google Docs, or coding unrelated to document generation. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["docx"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:import-memory","description":"Import a memory export from another AI assistant into Claude's memory — conversationally, additively, and with the content treated as data. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["import-memory"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:morning","description":"Render the user's morning brief as a styled HTML artifact, or set it up as a recurring weekday task. Use only when the user explicitly asks to run, see, or set up their morning brief, or if they invoke /morning by name. A question about their day, schedule, or calendar is not by itself a request for the brief; answer it directly instead. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["morning"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:pdf","description":"Use this skill whenever the user wants to do anything with PDF files. This includes reading or extracting text/tables from PDFs, combining or merging multiple PDFs into one, splitting PDFs apart, rotating pages, adding watermarks, creating new PDFs, filling PDF forms, encrypting/decrypting PDFs, extracting images, and OCR on scanned PDFs to make them searchable. If the user mentions a .pdf file or asks to produce one, use this skill. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["pdf"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:pptx","description":"Use this skill any time a .pptx or .potx file is involved in any way — as input, output, or both. This includes: creating slide decks, pitch decks, or presentations as PowerPoint (.pptx) files; reading, parsing, or extracting text from any .pptx or .potx file (even if the extracted content will be used elsewhere, like in an email, summary, or creating a different type of slide deck); editing, modifying, or updating existing presentations; combining or splitting slide files; working with templates (.potx), layouts, speaker notes, or comments. Trigger whenever the user asks for a PowerPoint or .pptx file, or references a .pptx or .potx filename, regardless of what they plan to do with the content afterward. However, when the user asks for a deck, slides, a slide deck, or a presentation without naming a file format, default to using a dedicated slide-deck artifact type or a separate slides skill if this session offers one; otherwise, use this skill. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["pptx"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:setup-writing-style","description":"Learns how the user writes from their own sent messages and docs, and builds a voice profile so future drafts sound like them instead of generic AI. The profile is saved as the my-writing-style skill. Use when the user asks to set up, learn, or capture their writing voice, or complains that drafts sound generic or unlike them and no my-writing-style profile exists. Only for drafting text the user will send as themselves, not for Claude's own replies. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["setup-writing-style"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:skill-creator","description":"Create new skills, modify and improve existing skills, and measure skill performance. Use when users want to create a skill from scratch, edit, or optimize an existing skill, run evals to test a skill, benchmark skill performance with variance analysis, or optimize a skill's description for better triggering accuracy. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["skill-creator"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:xlsx","description":"Use this skill any time a spreadsheet file is the primary input or output. This means any task where the user wants to: open, read, edit, or fix an existing .xlsx, .xlsm, .xltx, .csv, or .tsv file (e.g., adding columns, computing formulas, formatting, charting, cleaning messy data); create a new spreadsheet from scratch or from other data sources; or convert between tabular file formats. Trigger especially when the user references a spreadsheet file by name or path — even casually (like \"the xlsx in my downloads\") — and wants something done to it or produced from it. Also trigger for cleaning or restructuring messy tabular data files (malformed rows, misplaced headers, junk data) into proper spreadsheets. The deliverable must be a spreadsheet file. Do NOT trigger when the primary deliverable is a Word document, HTML report, standalone Python script, database pipeline, or Google Sheets API integration, even if tabular data is involved. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["xlsx"]}],"uuid":"cd028081-c089-45ce-88a4-d44ee8f16d45","session_id":"4a72212d-80e5-4847-a928-d550d7491d51"}

The running\n * service uses AlertMigrationRunnerService (wired in app.module.ts) which\n * executes migrations at OnApplicationBootstrap with search_path pinning\n * and per-migration transaction isolation.\n *\n * alert-engine follows the schema-per-tenant pattern: source tables live\n * in the `alert` schema; `CREATE TABLE LIKE INCLUDING ALL` provisions a\n * copy into each `tenant_<uuid>` schema at tenant onboarding. expect(align).toContain(\"'alert_rules'\");\n    expect(align).toContain(\"'escalation_policies'\");\n    expect(align).toContain(\"'alert_history'\");\n  });\n});\n\ndescribe('INVARIANT (INFRA-CRITICAL-030): tenant migration ledgers are source-schema namespaced', () => {\n  it('all tenant fan-out runners use tenantMigrationLedgerTable(sourceSchema)', () => {\n    // The deploy fan-out names the tenant ledger from the registry entry it is\n    // replaying; the ledger backfill (INFRA-CRITICAL-149) from its source\n    // schema. Both must go through the one helper.\n    expect(repoFile('apps/db-migrate/src/main.ts')).toContain(\n      'migrationsTableName: tenantMigrationLedgerTable(entry.schema)',\n    );\n    expect(repoFile('apps/db-migrate/src/tenant-ledger-backfill.ts')).toContain(\n      'tenantMigrationLedgerTable(sourceSchema)',\n    );\n    expect(repoFile('apps/db-migrate/src/tenant-schema-provisioner.ts')).toContain(\n      'tenantMigrationLedgerTable(entry.schema)',\n    );\n    expect(repoFile('libs/backend-common/src/database/schema-version-gate.service.ts')).toContain(\n      'tenantMigrationLedgerTable(sourceSchema)',\n    );\n  });\n\n  it('the SSoT helper rejects unsafe source schemas and keeps source ledgers canonical', () => {\n    const helper = repoFile('libs/backend-common/src/database/migration-ledger.ts');\n    expect(helper).toContain(\"export const MIGRATION_LEDGER_TABLE = 'migrations' as const\");\n    expect(helper).toContain('SAFE_SCHEMA_RE');\n    expect(helper).toContain('return `${MIGRATION_LEDGER_TABLE}_${sourceSchema}`');\n  });\n});\n\ndescribe('INVARIANT (INFRA-CRITICAL-031): HR drift is owned by db-migrate + migration harness', () => {\n  it('db-migrate registry owns HR migrations and HR entity metadata', () => {\n    const registry = repoFile('apps/db-migrate/src/schema-registry.ts');\n    expect(registry).toMatch(/service:\\s*'hr-service'[\\s\\S]*schema:\\s*'hr'/);\n    expect(registry).toContain('apps/hr-service/src/database/migrations/[0-9]*{.ts,.js}');\n    expect(registry).toContain('apps/hr-service/src/**/*.entity.{ts,js}');\n  });\n\n  it('the HR drift regression harness remains in the active migration-harness suite', () => {\n    const spec = repoFile(\n      'libs/migration-harness/src/__tests__/hr-drift-regression.integration.spec.ts',\n    );\n    expect(spec).toContain('hr');\n    expect(spec).toContain('expectNoDrift');\n    expect(spec).toContain('bootPostgresContainer');\n  });\n});\n\ndescribe('INVARIANT (INFRA-CRITICAL-029/032): admin and HR drift closure has owned gates', () => {\n  it('admin-api cross-schema entities are read views and SchemaDriftValidator skips them by contract', () => That made this gate fail on pre-existing\n248:      # (not just services that happen to own migrations).\n251:      # migration filename / class-name / timestamp-uniqueness\n261:  # init-scripts mounted, runs every service's TypeORM migration chain\n306:          key: db-migration-check-pg16-timescaledb-${{ hashFiles('apps/db-migrate/src/__tests__/bootstrap-from-scratch.spec.ts') }}\n358:  # migration/provisioning path, not a hand-rolled mock. Splitting also lets\n365:    # tenant-aware service's migration set into a fresh schema.\n576:  # migration-deletion-witness — pre-merge gate.\n578:  # Catches the silent-regression class where a migration file (or\n580:  # either a sibling-service migration carrying the same CREATE\n583:  # tools/gates/migration-deletion-witness.ts docblock for the full\n586:  migration-deletion-witness:\n607:      - name: Run migration-deletion-witness gate\n612:        run: npm run gates:migration-deletion-witness\n619:  # @Check change) WITHOUT a matching migration landing in the same PR.\n655:  # migration-immutability-witness — shipped-migration in-place-edit guard.\n658:  # migration file is edited in place instead of being superseded by a new\n659:  # forward migration. TypeORM records the ledger by migration NAME, so an\n660:  # edit to an already-applied migration never re-runs — the 2026-06 tenant-\n665:  # The PR-body waiver `MIGRATION-IMMUTABLE-OK: <filename> — <reason>` is\n669:  migration-immutability-witness:\n690:      - name: Run migration-immutability-witness gate\n694:        run: npm run gates:migration-immutability-witness -- --diff-base \"$BASE_SHA\"","numLines":70,"totalLines":70}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01GgC35NJpp2usJoTWBZZMNf","type":"tool_result","content":"255\t\n256\t  # ===================================================================\n257\t  # bootstrap-from-scratch — fresh-volume integration check.\n258\t  #\n259\t  # Runs the apps/db-migrate/src/__tests__/bootstrap-from-scratch.spec.ts test,\n260\t  # which spins up an empty Postgres testcontainer with the production\n261\t  # init-scripts mounted, runs every service's TypeORM migration chain\n262\t  # against it, and asserts the resulting schema state is what the\n263\t  # current entity surface expects.\n264\t  #\n265\t  # Why a separate job: this one needs a Docker daemon and ~5 minutes;\n266\t  # the conventions checks above are pure-shell <30s. Splitting keeps\n267\t  # PR feedback fast for the 99% case (filename/class-name regressions)\n268\t  # while still gating the rarer-but-catastrophic squash regression.\n269\t  #\n270\t  # GHA runners have docker preinstalled and the testcontainers library\n271\t  # autoreaches it; no extra setup beyond `npm ci`.\n272\t  # ===================================================================\n273\t  bootstrap-from-scratch:\n274\t    runs-on: ubuntu-latest\n275\t    # Wave 4-A.2 Dalga 5 — bootstrap-from-scratch is now ~8 minutes\n276\t    # because the entity-surface coverage matrix runs per service AND\n277\t    # the SchemaDriftValidator runtime gate (Part A.4) iterates each\n278\t    # service's metadata. 12-minute ceiling gives ~50% headroom while\n279\t    # keeping the zombie-job blast radius bounded.\n280\t    timeout-minutes: 12\n281\t\n282\t    steps:\n283\t      - name: Checkout repository\n284\t        uses: actions/checkout@3d3c42e5aac5ba805825da76410c181273ba90b1 # v7.0.1\n285\t\n286\t      - name: Setup Node.js\n287\t        uses: actions/setup-node@820762786026740c76f36085b0efc47a31fe5020 # v7.0.0\n288\t        with:\n289\t          node-version: '22'\n290\t          cache: 'npm'\n291\t\n292\t      - name: Install dependencies\n293\t        run: npm ci --prefer-offline --no-audit --ignore-scripts --no-fund\n294\t\n295\t      # Wave 4-A.2 Dalga 5 — testcontainers image cache extension.\n296\t      # The Postgres pull (~250 MB compressed) dominates cold-start\n297\t      # time; restoring the image tarball from a cache key shaves\n298\t      # 60-90 seconds off every CI run that touches a path-filtered\n299\t      # surface. Cache key bound to the pinned digest in the spec\n300\t      # so a digest bump invalidates the cache (correct behaviour).\n301\t      - name: Restore docker image cache\n302\t        id: docker-image-cache\n303\t        uses: actions/cache@55cc8345863c7cc4c66a329aec7e433d2d1c52a9 # v6.1.0\n304\t        with:\n305\t          path: /tmp/docker-image-cache\n306\t          key: db-migration-check-pg16-timescaledb-${{ hashFiles('apps/db-migrate/src/__tests__/bootstrap-from-scratch.spec.ts') }}\n307\t\n308\t      - name: Load cached image (if present)\n309\t        if: steps.docker-image-cache.outputs.cache-hit == 'true'\n310\t        run: |\n311\t          if [ -f /tmp/docker-image-cache/timescaledb-pg16.tar ]; then\n312\t            docker load -i /tmp/docker-image-cache/timescaledb-pg16.tar\n313\t          fi\n314\t\n315\t      - name: Run bootstrap-from-scratch test\n316\t        run: npm run test:bootstrap\n317\t        env:\n318\t          # testcontainers reuse cuts seconds off subsequent runs and is\n319\t          # safe in single-job CI (we don't reuse across jobs).\n320\t          TESTCONTAINERS_REUSE_ENABLE: 'true'\n321\t\n322\t      - name: Save docker image cache\n323\t        if: steps.docker-image-cache.outputs.cache-hit != 'true'\n324\t        run: |\n325\t          # Best-effort: save the timescaledb-ha image to the cache for\n326\t          # future runs. The grep|head pipeline can return empty if the\n327\t          # testcontainer cleaned up before this step runs (race), or\n328\t          # produce an `<none>:<none>` tag when an image is referenced\n329\t          # only by digest. Both yield `docker save` \"invalid reference\n330\t          # format\" and a non-zero exit code that fails the whole job.\n331\t          # The cache is an optimization, not a correctness gate — the\n332\t          # bootstrap test has already completed successfully by the\n333\t          # time this step runs. Guard the docker invocation so a cache\n334\t          # miss-save degrades to a no-op instead of failing CI.\n335\t          mkdir -p /tmp/docker-image-cache\n336\t          IMAGE_REF=$(docker images --format '{{.Repository}}:{{.Tag}}' \\\n337\t            | grep timescaledb-ha \\\n338\t            | grep -v '<none>' \\\n339\t            | head -1)\n340\t          if [ -z \"$IMAGE_REF\" ]; then\n341\t            echo \"No timescaledb-ha image with a usable tag found; skipping cache save.\"\n342\t            exit 0\n343\t          fi\n344\t          docker save -o /tmp/docker-image-cache/timescaledb-pg16.tar \"$IMAGE_REF\" \\\n345\t            || echo \"docker save failed for $IMAGE_REF; cache will be regenerated on next run.\"\n346\t\n347\t  # ===================================================================\n348\t  # tenant-clone-parity — Wave 4-A.2 Dalga 5 Part C.\n349\t  #\n350\t  # Asserts that per-tenant schema clones are a structurally faithful,\n351\t  # RLS-enabled mirror of every source-schema table. Runs against a\n352\t  # live Postgres service container (NOT testcontainers) after applying\n353\t  # the same aqua-db-migrate entrypoint used by production deploys.\n354\t  #\n355\t  # Why a separate job from bootstrap-from-scratch: the bootstrap spec\n356\t  # is hermetic (its own testcontainer); tenant-clone-parity targets\n357\t  # the running e2e Postgres so it can exercise the actual production\n358\t  # migration/provisioning path, not a hand-rolled mock. Splitting also lets\n359\t  # this job complete in ~3 minutes vs the bootstrap job's 8.\n360\t  # ===================================================================\n361\t  tenant-clone-parity:\n362\t    runs-on: ubuntu-latest\n363\t    # 15 -> 20: the job now also provisions a real tenant through the\n364\t    # production provisioner (DATA-CRITICAL-010), which replays every\n365\t    # tenant-aware service's migration set into a fresh schema.\n366\t    timeout-minutes: 20\n367\t    env:\n368\t      AQUA_TEST_DB_VALUE: aquaculture-test\n369\t\n370\t    services:\n371\t      postgres:\n372\t        image: timescale/timescaledb-ha:pg16@sha256:b3d038d0a0757df8a5ec0a94ba68d9ad57b0e16100a024cf4b370c77ad5645f7\n373\t        env:\n374\t          POSTGRES_DB: aquaculture\n375\t          POSTGRES_USER: aquaculture\n376\t          POSTGRES_PASSWORD: aquaculture-test\n377\t        ports:\n378\t          - 5432:5432\n379\t        options: >-\n380\t          --health-cmd \"pg_isready -U aquaculture\"\n381\t          --health-interval 10s\n382\t          --health-timeout 5s\n383\t          --health-retries 10\n384\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"4a72212d-80e5-4847-a928-d550d7491d51","uuid":"d07624f3-4029-4b72-943f-da3295c2be89","timestamp":"2026-09-19T16:17:18.743Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-evidence-judge-855f684571ae/.github/workflows/db-migration-check.yml","content":"\n  # ===================================================================\n  # bootstrap-from-scratch — fresh-volume integration check.\n  #\n  # Runs the apps/db-migrate/src/__tests__/bootstrap-from-scratch.spec.ts test,\n  # which spins up an empty Postgres testcontainer with the production\n  # init-scripts mounted, runs every service's TypeORM migration chain\n  # against it, and asserts the resulting schema state is what the\n  # current entity surface expects.\n  #\n  # Why a separate job: this one needs a Docker daemon and ~5 minutes;\n  # the conventions checks above are pure-shell <30s. Splitting keeps\n  # PR feedback fast for the 99% case (filename/class-name regressions)\n  # while still gating the rarer-but-catastrophic squash regression.\n  #\n  # GHA runners have docker preinstalled and the testcontainers library\n  # autoreaches it; no extra setup beyond `npm ci`.\n  # ===================================================================\n  bootstrap-from-scratch:\n    runs-on: ubuntu-latest\n    # Wave 4-A.2 Dalga 5 — bootstrap-from-scratch is now ~8 minutes\n    # because the entity-surface coverage matrix runs per service AND\n    # the SchemaDriftValidator runtime gate (Part A.4) iterates each\n    # service's metadata. 12-minute ceiling gives ~50% headroom while\n    # keeping the zombie-job blast radius bounded.\n    timeout-minutes: 12\n\n    steps:\n      - name: Checkout repository\n        uses: actions/checkout@3d3c42e5aac5ba805825da76410c181273ba90b1 # v7.0.1\n\n      - name: Setup Node.js\n        uses: actions/setup-node@820762786026740c76f36085b0efc47a31fe5020 # v7.0.0\n        with:\n          node-version: '22'\n          cache: 'npm'\n\n      - name: Install dependencies\n        run: npm ci --prefer-offline --no-audit --ignore-scripts --no-fund\n\n      # Wave 4-A.2 Dalga 5 — testcontainers image cache extension.\n      # The Postgres pull (~250 MB compressed) dominates cold-start\n      # time; restoring the image tarball from a cache key shaves\n      # 60-90 seconds off every CI run that touches a path-filtered\n      # surface. Cache key bound to the pinned digest in the spec\n      # so a digest bump invalidates the cache (correct behaviour).\n      - name: Restore docker image cache\n        id: docker-image-cache\n        uses: actions/cache@55cc8345863c7cc4c66a329aec7e433d2d1c52a9 # v6.1.0\n        with:\n          path: /tmp/docker-image-cache\n          key: db-migration-check-pg16-timescaledb-${{ hashFiles('apps/db-migrate/src/__tests__/bootstrap-from-scratch.spec.ts') }}\n\n      - name: Load cached image (if present)\n        if: steps.docker-image-cache.outputs.cache-hit == 'true'\n        run: |\n          if [ -f /tmp/docker-image-cache/timescaledb-pg16.tar ]; then\n            docker load -i /tmp/docker-image-cache/timescaledb-pg16.tar\n          fi\n\n      - name: Run bootstrap-from-scratch test\n        run: npm run test:bootstrap\n        env:\n          # testcontainers reuse cuts seconds off subsequent runs and is\n          # safe in single-job CI (we don't reuse across jobs).\n          TESTCONTAINERS_REUSE_ENABLE: 'true'\n\n      - name: Save docker image cache\n        if: steps.docker-image-cache.outputs.cache-hit != 'true'\n        run: |\n          # Best-effort: save the timescaledb-ha image to the cache for\n          # future runs. The grep|head pipeline can return empty if the\n          # testcontainer cleaned up before this step runs (race), or\n          # produce an `<none>:<none>` tag when an image is referenced\n          # only by digest. Both yield `docker save` \"invalid reference\n          # format\" and a non-zero exit code that fails the whole job.\n          # The cache is an optimization, not a correctness gate — the\n          # bootstrap test has already completed successfully by the\n          # time this step runs. Guard the docker invocation so a cache\n          # miss-save degrades to a no-op instead of failing CI.\n          mkdir -p /tmp/docker-image-cache\n          IMAGE_REF=$(docker images --format '{{.Repository}}:{{.Tag}}' \\\n            | grep timescaledb-ha \\\n            | grep -v '<none>' \\\n            | head -1)\n          if [ -z \"$IMAGE_REF\" ]; then\n            echo \"No timescaledb-ha image with a usable tag found; skipping cache save.\"\n            exit 0\n          fi\n          docker save -o /tmp/docker-image-cache/timescaledb-pg16.tar \"$IMAGE_REF\" \\\n            || echo \"docker save failed for $IMAGE_REF; cache will be regenerated on next run.\"\n\n  # ===================================================================\n  # tenant-clone-parity — Wave 4-A.2 Dalga 5 Part C.\n  #\n  # Asserts that per-tenant schema clones are a structurally faithful,\n  # RLS-enabled mirror of every source-schema table. Runs against a\n  # live Postgres service container (NOT testcontainers) after applying\n  # the same aqua-db-migrate entrypoint used by production deploys.\n  #\n  # Why a separate job from bootstrap-from-scratch: the bootstrap spec\n  # is hermetic (its own testcontainer); tenant-clone-parity targets\n  # the running e2e Postgres so it can exercise the actual production\n  # migration/provisioning path, not a hand-rolled mock. Splitting also lets\n  # this job complete in ~3 minutes vs the bootstrap job's 8.\n  # ===================================================================\n  tenant-clone-parity:\n    runs-on: ubuntu-latest\n    # 15 -> 20: the job now also provisions a real tenant through the\n    # production provisioner (DATA-CRITICAL-010), which replays every\n    # tenant-aware service's migration set into a fresh schema.\n    timeout-minutes: 20\n    env:\n      AQUA_TEST_DB_VALUE: aquaculture-test\n\n    services:\n      postgres:\n        image: timescale/timescaledb-ha:pg16@sha256:b3d038d0a0757df8a5ec0a94ba68d9ad57b0e16100a024cf4b370c77ad5645f7\n        env:\n          POSTGRES_DB: aquaculture\n          POSTGRES_USER: aquaculture\n          POSTGRES_PASSWORD: aquaculture-test\n        ports:\n          - 5432:5432\n        options: >-\n          --health-cmd \"pg_isready -U aquaculture\"\n          --health-interval 10s\n          --health-timeout 5s\n          --health-retries 10\n","numLines":130,"startLine":255,"totalLines":777}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_014RX2ZomzyeXT6YsQCeWFHB","type":"tool_result","content":"855\t      lockTimeoutSeconds: 60,\n856\t    });\n857\t\n858\t    // -----------------------------------------------------------------\n859\t    // 2. Run every service's migration chain in dependency order.\n860\t    // -----------------------------------------------------------------\n861\t    for (const svc of SERVICES) {\n862\t      const absDir = join(REPO_ROOT, svc.migrationsDir);\n863\t      const migrationClasses = loadMigrationClassesFromDir(absDir);\n864\t\n865\t      // Load entity classes for services that declare an entitiesGlob.\n866\t      // Required for migrations that introspect connection.entityMetadatas\n867\t      // — notably hr-service's SyncHrEntitiesToDb1786800000000 which\n868\t      // derives its DDL emit + entity-default registry from the entity\n869\t      // surface. Other services tolerate the empty-entities path (their\n870\t      // migrations are pure DDL). Services without entitiesGlob\n871\t      // (gateway-api) keep an empty list. Pairs with the\n872\t      // ALTER-COLUMN-TYPE default-recovery transform inside\n873\t      // SyncHrEntitiesToDb that prevents the \"default for column ...\n874\t      // cannot be cast automatically to type ..._enum\" failure mode on\n875\t      // fresh-volume bootstraps.\n876\t      const entityClasses: EntityClass[] = svc.entitiesGlob\n877\t        ? loadEntityClasses(join(REPO_ROOT, svc.entitiesGlob))\n878\t        : [];\n879\t\n880\t      const ds = new DataSource({\n881\t        type: 'postgres',\n882\t        host,\n883\t        port,\n884\t        username: DATABASE_USER,\n885\t        password: DATABASE_PASSWORD,\n886\t        database: DATABASE_NAME,\n887\t        schema: svc.schema,\n888\t        migrations: migrationClasses,\n889\t        entities: entityClasses,\n890\t        synchronize: false,\n891\t        migrationsRun: false,\n892\t        // Migration table inside the service schema; the production\n893\t        // MigrationRunnerService uses the same convention.\n894\t        migrationsTableName: 'migrations',\n895\t        // Pin search_path on every connection acquired from this pool.\n896\t        // Why: TypeORM's built-in MigrationExecutor (invoked by\n897\t        // ds.runMigrations() below) does NOT pin search_path —\n898\t        // production MigrationRunnerService does, but this test bypasses\n899\t        // the runner and goes straight to the executor. Migrations that\n900\t        // use unqualified table references (e.g. `ALTER TABLE \"users\"`,\n901\t        // `FROM \"users\"`) then resolve against the connection's default\n902\t        // search_path (`public`) and fail with `relation \"users\" does\n903\t        // not exist` on a fresh DB where auth tables only live in the\n904\t        // auth schema. The libpq `-c search_path=...` option sets the\n905\t        // path at session-start time, before any migration SQL runs,\n906\t        // and survives across the BEGIN/COMMIT cycles MigrationExecutor\n907\t        // uses in `transaction: 'each'` mode.\n908\t        extra: {\n909\t          options: `-c search_path=\"${svc.schema}\",public`,\n910\t        },\n911\t      });\n912\t\n913\t      try {\n914\t        await ds.initialize();\n915\t        // Skip runMigrations() for services with empty migration sets —\n916\t        // gateway-api owns no migrations dir and config-service ships\n917\t        // only `.gitkeep`. Calling runMigrations() with an empty list is\n918\t        // a no-op in TypeORM, but skipping the call avoids creating the\n919\t        // empty `<schema>.migrations` ledger table that would skew the\n920\t        // \"ledger is non-empty\" assertion below.\n921\t        if (migrationClasses.length > 0) {\n922\t          await runWithDbMigrateDdlAuthority(() => ds.runMigrations({ transaction: 'each' }));\n923\t        }\n924\t      } catch (err) {\n925\t        // Surface which service failed so the diagnostic message is\n926\t        // actionable from CI logs. Do not swallow — re-throw so Jest\n927\t        // marks the suite as failed.\n928\t        throw new Error(\n929\t          `[bootstrap-from-scratch] Migration run failed for service \"${svc.name}\" ` +\n930\t            `(schema=${svc.schema}, dir=${svc.migrationsDir}): ${(err as Error).message}\\n` +\n931\t            `${(err as Error).stack ?? ''}`,\n932\t        );\n933\t      }\n934\t      dataSources.set(svc.name, ds);"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"4a72212d-80e5-4847-a928-d550d7491d51","uuid":"7bb9c87a-a270-4191-867b-68337166bced","timestamp":"2026-09-19T16:17:35.761Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-evidence-judge-855f684571ae/apps/db-migrate/src/__tests__/bootstrap-from-scratch.spec.ts","content":"      lockTimeoutSeconds: 60,\n    });\n\n    // -----------------------------------------------------------------\n    // 2. Run every service's migration chain in dependency order.\n    // -----------------------------------------------------------------\n    for (const svc of SERVICES) {\n      const absDir = join(REPO_ROOT, svc.migrationsDir);\n      const migrationClasses = loadMigrationClassesFromDir(absDir);\n\n      // Load entity classes for services that declare an entitiesGlob.\n      // Required for migrations that introspect connection.entityMetadatas\n      // — notably hr-service's SyncHrEntitiesToDb1786800000000 which\n      // derives its DDL emit + entity-default registry from the entity\n      // surface. Other services tolerate the empty-entities path (their\n      // migrations are pure DDL). Services without entitiesGlob\n      // (gateway-api) keep an empty list. Pairs with the\n      // ALTER-COLUMN-TYPE default-recovery transform inside\n      // SyncHrEntitiesToDb that prevents the \"default for column ...\n      // cannot be cast automatically to type ..._enum\" failure mode on\n      // fresh-volume bootstraps.\n      const entityClasses: EntityClass[] = svc.entitiesGlob\n        ? loadEntityClasses(join(REPO_ROOT, svc.entitiesGlob))\n        : [];\n\n      const ds = new DataSource({\n        type: 'postgres',\n        host,\n        port,\n        username: DATABASE_USER,\n        password: DATABASE_PASSWORD,\n        database: DATABASE_NAME,\n        schema: svc.schema,\n        migrations: migrationClasses,\n        entities: entityClasses,\n        synchronize: false,\n        migrationsRun: false,\n        // Migration table inside the service schema; the production\n        // MigrationRunnerService uses the same convention.\n        migrationsTableName: 'migrations',\n        // Pin search_path on every connection acquired from this pool.\n        // Why: TypeORM's built-in MigrationExecutor (invoked by\n        // ds.runMigrations() below) does NOT pin search_path —\n        // production MigrationRunnerService does, but this test bypasses\n        // the runner and goes straight to the executor. Migrations that\n        // use unqualified table references (e.g. `ALTER TABLE \"users\"`,\n        // `FROM \"users\"`) then resolve against the connection's default\n        // search_path (`public`) and fail with `relation \"users\" does\n        // not exist` on a fresh DB where auth tables only live in the\n        // auth schema. The libpq `-c search_path=...` option sets the\n        // path at session-start time, before any migration SQL runs,\n        // and survives across the BEGIN/COMMIT cycles MigrationExecutor\n        // uses in `transaction: 'each'` mode.\n        extra: {\n          options: `-c search_path=\"${svc.schema}\",public`,\n        },\n      });\n\n      try {\n        await ds.initialize();\n        // Skip runMigrations() for services with empty migration sets —\n        // gateway-api owns no migrations dir and config-service ships\n        // only `.gitkeep`. Calling runMigrations() with an empty list is\n        // a no-op in TypeORM, but skipping the call avoids creating the\n        // empty `<schema>.migrations` ledger table that would skew the\n        // \"ledger is non-empty\" assertion below.\n        if (migrationClasses.length > 0) {\n          await runWithDbMigrateDdlAuthority(() => ds.runMigrations({ transaction: 'each' }));\n        }\n      } catch (err) {\n        // Surface which service failed so the diagnostic message is\n        // actionable from CI logs. Do not swallow — re-throw so Jest\n        // marks the suite as failed.\n        throw new Error(\n          `[bootstrap-from-scratch] Migration run failed for service \"${svc.name}\" ` +\n            `(schema=${svc.schema}, dir=${svc.migrationsDir}): ${(err as Error).message}\\n` +\n            `${(err as Error).stack ?? ''}`,\n        );\n      }\n      dataSources.set(svc.name, ds);","numLines":80,"startLine":855,"totalLines":1416}}}

A stitched\n380\t    envelope from a different lease (same request_id but different\n381\t    claim/agent) would pass.\n382\t    \"\"\"\n383\t    missing = [f for f in RESPONSE_REQUIRED_FIELDS if f raise GovernanceError(f\"agent-response $schema must be {RESPONSE_SCHEMA!r}\")\n388\t    if not isinstance(envelope[\"request_id\"], str) or not REQUEST_ID_RE.match(envelope[\"request_id\"]):\n389\t        raise GovernanceError(f\"agent-response.request_id invalid: {envelope['request_id']!r}\")\n390\t    claim_id = envelope[\"claim_id\"]\n391\t    if not isinstance(claim_id, str) or not CLAIM_ID_RE.match(claim_id):\n392\t        raise GovernanceError(f\"agent-response.claim_id invalid: {claim_id!r}\")\n393\t    agent_id = envelope[\"agent_id\"]\n394\t    if not isinstance(agent_id, str) or not AGENT_ID_RE.match(agent_id):\n395\t        raise GovernanceError(f\"agent-response.agent_id invalid: {agent_id!r}\")\n396\t    role = envelope[\"role\"]\n397\t    if role not in INVOCATION_ROLES:\n398\t        raise GovernanceError(f\"agent-response.role unknown: {role!r}\")\n399\t    status = envelope[\"status\"]\n400\t    if status not in RESPONSE_STATUSES:\n401\t        raise GovernanceError(f\"agent-response.status unknown: {status!r}\")\n402\t\n403\t    matrix = envelope[\"satisfaction_matrix\"]\n404\t    if not isinstance(matrix, list):\n405\t        raise GovernanceError(\"agent-response.satisfaction_matrix must be a list\")\n406\t    matrix_entries: list[dict[str, Any]] = []\n407\t    matrix_ids: set[str] = set()\n408\t    for idx, entry in enumerate(matrix):\n409\t        if not isinstance(entry, dict):\n410\t            raise GovernanceError(f\"satisfaction_matrix[{idx}] must be an object\")\n411\t        item_id = entry.get(\"id\")\n412\t        verdict = entry.get(\"verdict\")\n413\t        if not isinstance(item_id, str) or not item_id.strip():\n414\t            raise GovernanceError(f\"satisfaction_matrix[{idx}].id is required\")\n415\t        if item_id in matrix_ids:\n416\t            raise GovernanceError(f\"satisfaction_matrix[{idx}].id duplicate: {item_id}\")\n417\t        matrix_ids.add(item_id)\n418\t        if verdict not in SATISFACTION_VERDICTS:\n419\t            raise GovernanceError(\n420\t                f\"satisfaction_matrix[{idx}].verdict {verdict!r} not in {SATISFACTION_VERDICTS}\"\n421\t            )\n422\t        if verdict in {\"blocked\", \"contradicted\"}:\n423\t            note = entry.get(\"note\") or entry.get(\"rationale\") or \"\"\n424\t            if not isinstance(note, str) or not note.strip():\n425\t                raise GovernanceError(\n426\t                    f\"satisfaction_matrix[{idx}].note required when verdict={verdict!r}\"\n427\t                )\n428\t            _check_banned_phrases(note, field=f\"satisfaction_matrix[{item_id}].note\")\n429\t            evidence_refs = entry.get(\"evidence_refs\", [])\n430\t            _ensure_string_list(\n431\t                evidence_refs,\n432\t                field=f\"satisfaction_matrix[{item_id}].evidence_refs\",\n433\t                allow_empty=False,\n434\t            )\n435\t        matrix_entries.append(entry)\n436\t\n437\t    rationale = envelope.get(\"rationale\")\n438\t    if isinstance(rationale, str) and rationale.strip():\n439\t        _check_banned_phrases(rationale, field=\"agent-response.rationale\")\n440\t\n441\t    # Plan 023 v3 §A-5 — envelope claim/agent identity bound to lease.\n442\t    # When the caller supplies the leased claim+agent identity (which\n443\t    # submit_claim_result does), reject any envelope whose claim_id or\n444\t    # agent_id differs. Pre-fix a stitched envelope from a different\n445\t    # lease (same request_id but different claim/agent) passed because\n446\t    # only request_id was cross-checked.\n447\t    if lease is not None:\n448\t        leased_claim = lease.get(\"claim_id\")\n449\t        leased_agent = lease.get(\"agent_id\")\n450\t        if leased_claim is not None and envelope[\"claim_id\"] != leased_claim:\n451\t            raise GovernanceError(\n452\t                f\"envelope_claim_id_mismatch: envelope.claim_id=\"\n453\t                f\"{envelope['claim_id']!r} does not match leased claim_id=\"\n454\t                f\"{leased_claim!r}\"\n455\t            )\n456\t        if leased_agent is not None and envelope[\"agent_id\"] != leased_agent:\n457\t            raise GovernanceError(\n458\t                f\"envelope_agent_id_mismatch: envelope.agent_id=\"\n459\t                f\"{envelope['agent_id']!r} does not match leased agent_id=\""}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"4a72212d-80e5-4847-a928-d550d7491d51","uuid":"2da776e4-ab45-4805-a1cd-3946a18aac48","timestamp":"2026-09-19T16:19:02.291Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-evidence-judge-855f684571ae/aria-kernel/aria_kernel/agent_contract.py","content":"\n    `request` is optional — when provided, the response is cross-checked\n    against the request's `must_satisfy` ids and `expected_output_path`.\n\n    `lease` is optional — when provided (Plan 023 v3 §A-5), the\n    envelope's claim_id and agent_id MUST match the leased identity.\n    Pre-Plan-023 the envelope's claim_id was only validated for SHAPE\n    (CLAIM_ID_RE pattern) and the request_id was matched to the\n    request, but envelope.claim_id and envelope.agent_id were never\n    bound to the lease that authorized the submission. A stitched\n    envelope from a different lease (same request_id but different\n    claim/agent) would pass.\n    \"\"\"\n    missing = [f for f in RESPONSE_REQUIRED_FIELDS if f not in envelope]\n    if missing:\n        raise GovernanceError(f\"agent-response missing required fields: {missing}\")\n    if envelope[\"$schema\"] != RESPONSE_SCHEMA:\n        raise GovernanceError(f\"agent-response $schema must be {RESPONSE_SCHEMA!r}\")\n    if not isinstance(envelope[\"request_id\"], str) or not REQUEST_ID_RE.match(envelope[\"request_id\"]):\n        raise      raise GovernanceError(f\"agent-response.claim_id invalid: {claim_id!r}\")\n    agent_id = envelope[\"agent_id\"]\n    if not isinstance(agent_id, str) or not AGENT_ID_RE.match(agent_id):\n        raise GovernanceError(f\"agent-response.agent_id invalid: {agent_id!r}\")\n    role = envelope[\"role\"]\n    if role not in INVOCATION_ROLES:\n        raise GovernanceError(f\"agent-response.role unknown: {role!r}\")\n    status = envelope[\"status\"]\n    if status not in RESPONSE_STATUSES:\n        raise GovernanceError(f\"agent-response.status unknown: {status!r}\")\n\n    matrix = envelope[\"satisfaction_matrix\"]\n    if not isinstance(matrix, list):\n        raise GovernanceError(\"agent-response.satisfaction_matrix must be a list\")\n    matrix_entries: list[dict[str, Any]] = []\n    matrix_ids: set[str] = set()\n    for idx, entry in enumerate(matrix):\n        if not isinstance(entry, dict):\n            raise GovernanceError(f\"satisfaction_matrix[{idx}] must be an object\")\n        item_id = entry.get(\"id\")\n        verdict = entry.get(\"verdict\")\n        if not isinstance(item_id, str) or not item_id.strip():\n            raise GovernanceError(f\"satisfaction_matrix[{idx}].id is required\")\n        if item_id in matrix_ids:\n            raise GovernanceError(f\"satisfaction_matrix[{idx}].id duplicate: {item_id}\")\n        matrix_ids.add(item_id)\n        if verdict not in SATISFACTION_VERDICTS:\n            raise GovernanceError(\n                f\"satisfaction_matrix[{idx}].verdict {verdict!r} not in {SATISFACTION_VERDICTS}\"\n            )\n        if verdict in {\"blocked\", \"contradicted\"}:\n            note = entry.get(\"note\") or entry.get(\"rationale\") or \"\"\n            if not isinstance(note, str) or not note.strip():\n                raise GovernanceError(\n                    f\"satisfaction_matrix[{idx}].note required when verdict={verdict!r}\"\n                )\n            _check_banned_phrases(note, field=f\"satisfaction_matrix[{item_id}].note\")\n            evidence_refs = entry.get(\"evidence_refs\", [])\n            _ensure_string_list(\n                evidence_refs,\n                field=f\"satisfaction_matrix[{item_id}].evidence_refs\",\n                allow_empty=False,\n            )\n        matrix_entries.append(entry)\n\n    rationale = envelope.get(\"rationale\")\n    if isinstance(rationale, str) and rationale.strip():\n        _check_banned_phrases(rationale, field=\"agent-response.rationale\")\n\n    # Plan 023 v3 §A-5 — envelope claim/agent identity bound to lease.\n    # When the caller supplies the leased claim+agent identity (which\n    # submit_claim_result does), reject any envelope whose claim_id or\n    # agent_id differs. Pre-fix a stitched envelope from a different\n    # lease (same request_id but different claim/agent) passed because\n    # only request_id was cross-checked.\n    if lease is not None:\n        leased_claim = lease.get(\"claim_id\")\n        leased_agent = lease.get(\"agent_id\")\n        if leased_claim is not None and envelope[\"claim_id\"] != leased_claim:\n            raise GovernanceError(\n                f\"envelope_claim_id_mismatch: envelope.claim_id=\"\n                f\"{envelope['claim_id']!r} does not match leased claim_id=\"\n                f\"{leased_claim!r}\"\n            )\n        if leased_agent is not None and envelope[\"agent_id\"] != leased_agent:\n            raise GovernanceError(\n                f\"envelope_agent_id_mismatch: envelope.agent_id=\"\n                f\"{envelope['agent_id']!r} does not match leased agent_id=\"","numLines":90,"startLine":370,"totalLines":616}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_0171SsUcHxgSsQAJQfkrJbMH","type":"tool_result","content":"2228\t    # Plan ARIA-V7 §2g v2 + V7.10 envelope-extraction fix.\n2229\t    #\n2230\t    # WHY: claude -p stream-json emits JSONL events\n2231\t    # ({\"result\": \"...\", \"total_cost_usd\": ..., ...}) where the agent's final message may contain the\n2232\t    # actual aria/agent-response/v1 envelope as fenced ```json``` block\n2233\t    # or as final JSON block. Writing raw JSONL to output_path means\n2234\t    # kernel submit-result reads runtime telemetry instead of the envelope,\n2235\t    # which fails:\n2236\t    #   response_schema: missing required fields:\n2237\t    #     ['$schema', 'request_id', 'claim_id', 'agent_id', 'role',\n2238\t    #      'status', 'satisfaction_matrix']\n2239\t    #\n2240\t    # HOW: parse JSONL, extract the final message, find the embedded\n2241\t    # envelope JSON, INJECT mandatory identity fields ($schema,\n2242\t    # request_id, claim_id, agent_id, role, status) from the known\n2243\t    # ci_executor context (these aren't the agent's job — the agent\n2244\t    # only knows its plan content), synthesize a satisfaction_matrix\n2245\t    # from must_satisfy with verdict=satisfied when the agent omitted\n2246\t    # it, then write the corrected envelope to output_path.\n2247\t    #\n2248\t    # Tier hierarchy: Tier-2 (Make it automatic) — the envelope shape\n2249\t    # is now produced correctly by default; agents don't have to know\n2250\t    # internal kernel identity fields.\n2251\t    if completed.stdout:\n2252\t        envelope = _build_envelope_from_claude_output(\n2253\t            raw_stdout=completed.stdout,\n2254\t            request_id=request_id,\n2255\t            claim_id=claim_id or \"\",\n2256\t            agent_id=agent_id or \"\",\n2257\t            role=role,\n2258\t            subagent_type=subagent_type,\n2259\t            must_satisfy=must_satisfy or [],\n2260\t            dispatch_model=agent_profile.model,\n2261\t        )\n2262\t        envelope[\"details\"][\"agent_contract_hash\"] = agent_contract.contract_hash\n2263\t        output_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n2264\t        _write_sanitized_envelope(output_path, envelope)\n2265\t        resolved_transcript_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n2266\t        resolved_transcript_path.write_text(completed.stdout, encoding=\"utf-8\")\n2267\t        # Plan ARIA-V3.1-D3 — per-LLM-call cost attribution. Gated on"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"4a72212d-80e5-4847-a928-d550d7491d51","uuid":"a1fc9114-1b63-4dfa-9fa8-d984d59c2a0d","timestamp":"2026-09-19T16:19:28.909Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-evidence-judge-855f684571ae/tools/aria-poc/ci_executor.py","content":"    # Plan ARIA-V7 §2g v2 + V7.10 envelope-extraction fix.\n    #\n    # WHY: claude -p stream-json emits JSONL events\n    # ({\"result\": \"...\", \"total_cost_usd\": ..., ...}) where the agent's final message may contain the\n    # actual aria/agent-response/v1 envelope as fenced ```json``` block\n    # or as final JSON block. Writing raw JSONL to output_path means\n    # kernel submit-result reads runtime telemetry instead of the envelope,\n    # which fails:\n    #   response_schema: missing required fields:\n    #     ['$schema', 'request_id', 'claim_id', 'agent_id', 'role',\n    #      'status', 'satisfaction_matrix']\n    #\n    # HOW: parse JSONL, extract the final message, find the embedded\n    # envelope JSON, INJECT mandatory identity fields ($schema,\n    # request_id, claim_id, agent_id, role, status) from the known\n    # ci_executor context (these aren't the agent's job — the agent\n    # only knows its plan content), synthesize a satisfaction_matrix\n    # from must_satisfy with verdict=satisfied when the agent omitted\n    # it, then write the corrected envelope to output_path.\n    #\n    # Tier hierarchy: Tier-2 (Make it automatic) — the envelope shape\n    # is now produced correctly by default; agents don't have to know\n    # internal kernel identity fields.\n    if completed.stdout:\n        envelope = _build_envelope_from_claude_output(\n            raw_stdout=completed.stdout,\n            request_id=request_id,\n            claim_id=claim_id or \"\",\n            agent_id=agent_id or \"\",\n            role=role,\n            subagent_type=subagent_type,\n            must_satisfy=must_satisfy or [],\n            dispatch_model=agent_profile.model,\n        )\n        envelope[\"details\"][\"agent_contract_hash\"] = agent_contract.contract_hash\n        output_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n        _write_sanitized_envelope(output_path, envelope)\n        resolved_transcript_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n        resolved_transcript_path.write_text(completed.stdout, encoding=\"utf-8\")\n        # Plan ARIA-V3.1-D3 — per-LLM-call cost attribution. {key[1]}\")\n        if key in seen:\n            raise GovernanceError(f\"duplicate batch verdict for sample item: {key[0]} {key[1]}\")\n        seen.add(key)\n        normalized.append((verdict, by_key[key]))\n    missing = sorted(set(by_key) - seen)\n    if missing:\n        first = missing[0]\n        raise GovernanceError(f\"batch verdict missing sample item: {first[0]} {first[1]}\")\n\n    rows = []\n    for verdict, item in normalized:\n        rows.append(\n            record_operator_feedback(\n                tool_id=str(item.get(\"tool_id\") or sample.get(\"tool_id\") or \"\"),\n                run_id=str(item.get(\"run_id\") or \"\"),\n                finding_id=str(item.get(\"finding_id\") or \"\"),\n                verdict=str(verdict.get(\"verdict\") or \"\"),\n                severity=str(verdict.get(\"severity\") or item.get(\"severity\") or \"medium\"),\n                note=str(verdict.get(\"note\") or verdict.get(\"rationale\") or \"batch operator verdict\"),\n                affected_belief_ids=_optional_string_list(verdict.get(\"affected_belief_ids\")),\n                evidence_refs=_optional_string_list(verdict.get(\"evidence_refs\")) or _evidence_refs_from_item(item),\n                rationale=str(verdict.get(\"rationale\") or \"\"),\n                judgment_group_id=str(verdict.get(\"judgment_group_id\") or sample_id),\n                finding_fingerprint=str(item.get(\"finding_fingerprint\") or \"\"),\n                base_dir=base_dir,\n            ),\n        )\n    stored_sample = dict(sample)\n    stored_sample[\"status\"] = \"recorded\"\n    stored_sample[\"recorded_feedback_count\"] = len(rows)\n    return {\"schema_version\": 1, \"sample_id\": sample_id, \"recorded_count\": len(rows), \"feedback\": rows}\n\n\ndef record_ai_feedback_file(\n    *,\n    file_payload: Any,\n    base_dir: str | Path | None = None,\n) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    verdicts = file_payload.get(\"verdicts\") if isinstance(file_payload, dict) else file_payload\n    if not isinstance(verdicts, list):\n        raise GovernanceError(\"AI verdict file must be an array or an object with verdicts array\")\n    rows = []\n    for verdict in verdicts:\n        if not isinstance(verdict, dict):\n            raise GovernanceError(\"AI verdict entries must be JSON objects\")\n        rows.append(\n            record_operator_feedback(\n                tool_id=str(verdict.get(\"tool_id\") or \"\"),\n                run_id=str(verdict.get(\"run_id\") or \"\"),\n                finding_id=str(verdict.get(\"finding_id\") or \"\"),\n                verdict=str(verdict.get(\"verdict\") or \"\"),\n                severity=str(verdict.get(\"severity\") or \"medium\"),\n                note=str(verdict.get(\"note\") or verdict.get(\"rationale\") or \"\"),\n                affected_belief_ids=_optional_string_list(verdict.get(\"affected_belief_ids\")),\n                source_type=\"ai_judge\",\n                judge_id=str(verdict.get(\"judge_id\") or \"\"),\n                model=str(verdict.get(\"model\") or \"\"),\n                prompt_hash=str(verdict.get(\"prompt_hash\") or \"\"),\n                confidence=float(verdict.get(\"confidence\", 0.0)),\n                rationale=str(verdict.get(\"rationale\") or \"\"),\n                evidence_refs=_optional_string_list(verdict.get(\"evidence_refs\")),\n                judgment_group_id=str(verdict.get(\"judgment_group_id\") or \"\"),\n                finding_fingerprint=str(verdict.get(\"finding_fingerprint\") or \"\"),\n                base_dir=base_dir,\n            ),\n        )\n    return {\"schema_version\": 1, \"recorded_count\": len(rows), \"feedback\": rows}\n\n\ndef _has_unverifiable_evidence(rows: list[dict[str, Any]], workspace_root: str | Path) -> bool:\n    \"\"\"Plan 024 §C — True if any judge in the group cites an evidence ref that\n    positively does not resolve in the repo (``missing`` / ``invalid``). A ref\n    that exists but is unverified at a pinned sha (``worktree_candidate``) is\n    given the benefit of the doubt — this gate catches fabricated evidence, not","numLines":100,"startLine":630,"totalLines":1594}}}

Hazard is confirmed from the file itself: CREATE TYPE/TABLE/INDEX and an FK (lines 8-43), tenant RLS install (46-49), an append-only trigger plus REVOKE on alert.alert_audit_log (52-67), and a down() that drops every table (86-131). Adjacent coverage is genuinely absent: the only spec under apps/alert-engine/src/database/ is entities/__tests__/alert-incident.entity.spec.ts. Importing coverage exists and executes the migration: apps/db-migrate/src/__tests__/bootstrap-from-scratch.spec.ts lists alert-engine with migrationsDir apps/alert-engine/src/database/migrations (261-263); loadMigrationClassesFromDir (448-486) matches 1800000000000-Baseline.ts by filename regex (469) and requires the module at runtime (482), selecting the Baseline1800000000000 class; the per-service loop (861-934) runs ds.runMigrations({transaction:'each'}) on a fresh Postgres testcontainer with search_path pinned to alert and re-throws naming the service on failure; the assertion 'alert.alert_rules exists (alert-engine baseline)' (1190-1196) probes information_schema for the table created at Baseline line 9; ledger parity (1254) fails if the Baseline did not record. This suite is a CI gate: package.json:125 defines test:bootstrap, .github/workflows/db-migration-check.yml runs it in the bootstrap-from-scratch job (273-320) with a path filter on apps/*/src/**/migrations/** (91), so any edit to this file triggers it. A second, static signal keyed on this exact path: tests/invariants/critical-infra-ssot.spec.ts:208-230 reads the file and asserts the alert_incidents tenant_id uuid NOT NULL and alert.alert_audit_log CREATE TABLE statements. Why the adapter missed it: this repository never statically imports migrations — apps/alert-engine/src/database/data-source.ts:29 and apps/db-migrate/src/schema-registry.ts:258 load them by directory glob, and the bootstrap spec does the same.
